I'm not quite sure about having trouble organizing, but those other two are definitely major issues for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often feel restless, fidget a lot, interrupt others, or act before thinking, or are your problems mainly with inattention?\n\nPatient: My problems are definitely almost entirely with inattention rather than restlessness or impulsivity. I don't really fidget or interrupt people, but I constantly make careless mistakes, avoid mentally draining tasks, and lose important things.\n\nDoctor: Are these attention problems causing significant trouble in more than one area of your life, such as work, school, home responsibilities, or relationships?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I'm not quite sure about that. I haven't really thought about it in that way, I just know it has been really frustrating to deal with lately.\n\nDoctor: Has this been causing clear problems at work or school, at home, or in your relationships, even if you have not thought about it that way before?\n\nPatient: I'm still not entirely sure, as I haven't really thought about whether it has caused clear, major problems in those specific areas yet. Right now, I'm just mostly aware of how frustrating it is for me personally to deal with every day.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me one or two specific examples of how these attention problems have affected your work, school, home life, or relationships?\n\nPatient: At work, I often put off writing big reports because they require so much mental focus, and when I finally do them, I make careless mistakes like entering the wrong numbers. At home, I constantly misplace my keys and wallet, which makes me late because I have to spend twenty minutes frantically searching for them.\n\nDoctor: Attention-Deficit/Hyperactivity Disorder (Predominantly Inattentive Presentation)\n\nPatient: That diagnosis sounds incredibly accurate to me. I've been struggling with making careless mistakes, avoiding mentally draining tasks, and constantly losing my things for about a year and a half now. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.
